=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer array nums and two integers k and numOperations.
You must perform an operation numOperations times on nums, where in each operation you:

Select an index i that was not selected in any previous operations.
Add an integer in the range [-k, k] to nums[i].

Return the maximum possible frequency of any element in nums after performing the operations.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [1,4,5], k = 1, numOperations = 2
Output: 2
Explanation:
We can achieve a maximum frequency of two by:

Adding 0 to nums[1]. nums becomes [1, 4, 5].
Adding -1 to nums[2]. nums becomes [1, 4, 4].


Example 2:

Input: nums = [5,11,20,20], k = 5, numOperations = 1
Output: 2
Explanation:
We can achieve a maximum frequency of two by:

Adding 0 to nums[1].


 
Constraints:

1 <= nums.length <= 10^5
1 <= nums[i] <= 10^5
0 <= k <= 10^5
0 <= numOperations <= nums.length

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maxFrequency(self, nums: List[int], k: int, numOperations: int) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's write code:

```
from typing import List
from collections import Counter
from bisect import bisect_left, bisect_right

class Solution:
    def maxFrequency(self, nums: List[int], k: int, numOperations: int) -> int:
        nums.sort()
        cnt = Counter(nums)
        candidates = set(nums)
        for v in nums:
            candidates.add(v - k)
            candidates.add(v + k)
        ans = 0
        for x in candidates:
            total = bisect_right(nums, x + k) - bisect_left(nums, x - k)
            same = cnt.get(x, 0)
            ans = max(ans, min(total, same + numOperations))
        return ans
```

Is this enough? Let's test with example1:

Now, let's maybe derive a proof of formula to be comfortable:
For a fixed target value x, let S be set of indices whose final value is x. Any index not originally x must be selected and changed, consuming one operation. Any index originally x can be left unselected, or selected with +0 if needed. Thus if C = count original x, and T = count original values within k of x, the maximum number of final x is C + min(numOperations, T-C) = min(T, C+numOperations). Because only T elements can possibly become x; among them C already are x, and at most numOperations of the remaining T-C can be changed. Achievable by changing any min(numOperations, T-C) of the remaining elements and leaving C unchanged; waste extra operations with +0. Good.

Now, candidate set proof:
T changes only when x passes y-k or y+k for some original value y. C changes only when x equals y. Between consecutive such critical points, both T and C are constant. At a critical point, T is at least as large as on the adjacent interval(s), and C may be positive. Since answer formula is nondecreasing in both T and C, maximum occurs at a critical point. Thus candidates y, y-k, y+k. Need be careful "nondecreasing in both T and C" with formula min(T, C+numOps): yes, increasing T or C cannot decrease min. At critical point T >= adjacent interval T, C >= adjacent C (C=0 on open interval, at critical if y, C positive; if boundary not y, C=0). So max at critical. Good.

Actually, is T at critical point always >= adjacent interval T on both sides? Let's verify with critical point a = y+k. For x just left of a, y is included; at x=a, y included; for x just right, y excluded. So T(a) >= T(right), T(a) = T(left). For a = y-k. For x just right, y included; at x=a, y included; for x just left, y excluded. So T(a) >= T(left), T(a)=T(right). For a = y. For x on both sides, y is included? If x<y, y > x, need y <= x+k? If x close to y, yes if x > y-k. But the adjacent open intervals may be between y-k and y, and between y and y+k. On both, y is included because x in (y-k, y+k). At x=y, y included. So T same. Thus T(a) >= both adjacent intervals. Good. So maximum over closed intervals at critical points.

Now, let's think about if critical points set should include y-k and y+k even if k=0; duplicates. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with using `cnt.get(x, 0)` for x not in original but x maybe in candidate set as v-k and v+k. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the starter code not importing List; we'll include import. In LeetCode, they often don't require imports? But if we include, fine. The final answer should be code block only. We'll include:
```
from typing import List
from collections import Counter
from bisect import bisect_left, bisect_right

class Solution:
    def maxFrequency(self, nums: List[int], k: int, numOperations: int) -> int:
        nums.sort()
        cnt = Counter(nums)
        candidates = set(cnt.keys())
        for v in cnt:
            candidates.add(v - k)
            candidates.add(v + k)
        ans = 0
        for x in candidates:
            total = bisect_right(nums, x + k) - bisect_left(nums, x - k)
            same = cnt.get(x, 0)
            ans = max(ans, min(total, same + numOperations))
        return ans
```
Is there any issue with iterating over `cnt` while adding to candidates? We don't modify cnt, only candidates. Fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums" length 1e5 and "numOperations" = n-1 and nums all distinct but k large enough, answer n-1? Wait if numOps=n-1 and no existing target, can we get n? Need n elements to become target, but only n-1 operations, no existing target, so max n-1. But if target equals one of the elements, then same=1 and need change n-1 others, numOps=n-1, answer n. Example nums=[1,2,3], k=1, numOps=2, target2, change1 and3 to2, answer3. Code x=2 total3 same1 f=min(3,3)=3. Good. So answer can be n even if numOps=n-1 if there is an existing target. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums" length 1e5 and "numOperations" = n-1 and nums all distinct but k large enough, answer n-1? Wait if numOps=n-1 and no existing target, can we get n? Need n elements to become target, but only n-1 operations, no existing target, so max n-1. But if target equals one of the elements, then same=1 and need change n-1 others, numOps=n-1, answer n. Example nums=[1,2,3], k=1, numOps=2, target2, change1 and3 to2, answer3. Code x=2 total3 same1 f=min(3,3)=3. Good. So answer can be n even if numOps=n-1 if there is an existing target. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums" length 1e5 and "numOperations" = n-1 and nums all distinct but k large enough, answer n-1? Wait if numOps=n-1 and no existing target, can we get n? Need n elements to become target, but only n-1 operations, no existing target, so max n-1. But if target equals one of the elements, then same=1 and need change n-1 others, numOps=n-1, answer n. Example nums=[1,2,3], k=1, numOps=2, target2, change1 and3 to2, answer3. Code x=2 total3 same1 f=min(3,3)=3. Good. So answer can be n even if numOps=n-1 if there is an existing target. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
format. The user asked: "Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block." So final should be code block only.

But before final, let's continue reasoning to ensure no hidden issue.

Let's think about if candidate set of `nums[i]`, `nums[i]-k`, `nums[i]+k` is enough for formula `min(total, same+numOps)`. We argued yes. But let's test a tricky case where optimal x is not in candidate set and same=0, total high, but candidate boundary has same positive and total high, formula maybe lower? Wait formula nondecreasing in total and same, so if boundary has total >= interior and same >=0, f >= interior. Good.

But is total at boundary always >= interior on both adjacent intervals? Let's prove with an example where critical point is a nums value y, and adjacent intervals (y-k, y) and (y, y+k). For x in (y-k, y), y is included? Condition y <= x+k? Since x < y, x+k could be < y if x < y-k, but x > y-k, so x+k > y, yes included. For x in (y, y+k), y is included? Need y >= x-k? x < y+k => x-k < y, yes. So y included in both adjacent intervals. At x=y, y included. So total same. For critical point y-k: For x in (y-k, y), y included. At x=y-k, y included. For x in (y-2k, y-k), y not included? If x < y-k, then x+k < y, so y not included. At x=y-k, y included, so total jumps up. Thus T(endpoint) >= T(open interval) for both adjacent? For left interval (y-2k, y-k), T(endpoint) > T(left). For right interval (y-k, y), T(endpoint) = T(right). Good. For critical point y+k: symmetric. So yes.

Now, what about if k=0, critical points all same, no open intervals. Fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "same" at boundary if boundary is not a nums value but equals y-k or y+k; same=0. If boundary is a nums value, same positive. Good.
